(6.65)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\Gamma\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\right)s}{\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}\sqrt{\frac{K}{t-s}}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}.

Furthermore, notice that

P_{s}q(t,x)-q(t,x)=\int_{0}^{s}GP_{\tau}q(t,x)\,d\tau=\int_{0}^{s}P_{\tau}Gq(t,x)\,d\tau

where we used Item
(3)
of Lemma
6.9
since
q(t,\cdot),\partial_{x}q(t,\cdot)
and
Gq(t,\cdot)
belong to
\cC_{0}
. In particular, recall that, by (
6.47
), that
Gq(t,\cdot)\in\cC_{0}(1/2)
and

[Gq(t,\cdot)]_{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{K}{2t}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}.

Hence, by Item
(5)
of Lemma
6.9
we get, since
\tau\leq s\leq t

\displaystyle|P_{\tau}Gq(t,x)|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{K}{2t}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{3}{8}\tau+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{K}{2t}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}

and then

(6.66)

|P_{s}q(t,x)-q(t,x)|\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{K}{2t}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}s

Analogously,

(6.67)

|P_{s}q(t_{n},x)-q(t_{n},x)|\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{K}{2t}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}s.

Combining (
6.64
), (
6.65
), (
6.66
) and (
6.67
) with (
6.63
) we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|P_{s}q(t-s,x)&-q(t,x)-P_{s}q(t_{n}-s,x)+q(t_{n},x)|\\
&\leq\frac{4\Gamma\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\right)s}{\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}\sqrt{\frac{K}{t-s}}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}+4\sqrt{\frac{K}{2t}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}s.\end{split}

Integrating the latter against
s^{-1-\alpha}
we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}|P_{s}q(t-s,x)&-q(t,x)-P_{s}q(t_{n}-s,x)+q(t_{n},x)|s^{-1-\alpha}\,ds\\
&\leq\frac{4\Gamma\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\right)}{\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}\sqrt{K}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}s^{-\alpha}\,ds\\
&\qquad+4\sqrt{\frac{K}{2t}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}s^{-\alpha}\,ds\\
&=\frac{4\Gamma\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\Gamma(\alpha)\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-\alpha\right)}\sqrt{\frac{K}{\pi}}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\\
&\qquad+4\sqrt{\frac{K}{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\pi(1-\alpha)\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\end{split}

where we also used
[
3
, Lemma 1.11]
. Hence, we can use the dominated convergence theorem to guarantee that

\lim_{n\to\infty}I_{n}(x)=0.

Next, we move to
J_{n}(x)
. We have, arguing as before and recalling that this time
s\in[t_{n},t]
,

\displaystyle|P_{s}q(t_{n}-s,x)-q(t_{n},x)|

\displaystyle\leq|P_{s}q(t_{n}-s,x)-P_{s}q(t_{n},x)|+|P_{s}q(t_{n},x)-q(t_{n},x)|

\displaystyle\leq P_{s}|q(t_{n}-s,\cdot)-q(t_{n},\cdot)|+|P_{s}q(t_{n},x)-q(t_{n},x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\Gamma\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\right)s}{\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}\sqrt{\frac{K}{t_{n}-s}}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}+2\sqrt{\frac{K}{2t}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}s.

Hence,

\displaystyle J_{n}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\Gamma\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\right)}{\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}\sqrt{K}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}\int_{t}^{t_{n}}(t_{n}-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}s^{-\alpha}\,ds+2\sqrt{\frac{K}{2t}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}\int_{t}^{t_{n}}s^{-\alpha}ds

\displaystyle=\frac{2\Gamma\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\right)}{\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}\sqrt{K}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}\int_{0}^{t_{n}-t}w^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{n}-w)^{-\alpha}\,dw+2\sqrt{\frac{K}{2t}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\pi(1-\alpha)\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}(t_{n}^{1-\alpha}-t^{1-\alpha})

\displaystyle=\frac{2\Gamma\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\right)}{\pi\Gamma(\alpha)}\sqrt{K}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}t_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\int_{0}^{1-\frac{t}{t_{n}}}z^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-z)^{-\alpha}\,dz+2\sqrt{\frac{K}{2t}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\pi(1-\alpha)\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{3}{8}t_{1}+\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}(t_{n}^{1-\alpha}-t^{1-\alpha}).

Hence, taking the limit as
n\to\infty
, we get

\lim_{n\to\infty}J_{n}(x)=0.

Combined with the fact that also the remaining terms converge to 0 as
n\to\infty
, this finally shows that

\lim_{n\to\infty}(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q(t_{n},x)=(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q(t,x).

Now we prove that
\mathcal{I}_{x}=\varnothing
for all
x>0
. Indeed, fix
x>0
and assume that
\mathcal{I}_{x}\not=\varnothing
. Let
t\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
and consider an interval of the form
[t,t+\delta]
for some
\delta>0
. Since
|\mathcal{I}_{x}\cap[t,t+\delta]|=0
, we know that there exists a sequence
t_{n}\downarrow t
such that
t_{n}\not\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
for all
n\in\N
. For such a sequence, we know that

(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q(t_{n},x)=\frac{t_{n}^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}q(0,x).

Taking the limit as
n\to\infty
we get

(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q(t,x)=\frac{t^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}q(0,x)

that is absurd. Hence
\mathcal{I}_{x}=\varnothing
and thus we get that

(6.68)

(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q(t,x)=\frac{t^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}q(0,x),\ \qquad\forall t,x>0.

Finally, we notice that
q\in\cC_{0,{\rm loc}}(\R_{0}^{+})
by Proposition
6.15
.
∎

Remark 6.20
.

Actually, the first equality in (
6.36
) guarantees that
(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q
is continuous in both variables.

6.2.3.
Uniqueness of the solution

In order to prove that (
6.36
) has a unique solution, we need to consider an auxiliary problem. For
\beta\geq 0
consider the function
f_{\beta}(x)=e^{\beta|\log(x)|}
and, by Item
(4)
of Lemma
6.9
,

\displaystyle u_{\beta}(t,x)=P_{t}f_{\beta}(x)

\displaystyle=e^{\beta\log(x)+\frac{\beta(\beta-1)}{2}t}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}-\frac{\log(x)}{\sqrt{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+e^{-\beta\log(x)+\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta+\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}+\frac{\log(x)}{\sqrt{t}}\right).

Let us give some preliminary estimates on
u_{\beta}
, that can be verified by direct evaluation.

Lemma 6.21
.

For all
\beta\geq 0
and
t,x>0
we have:

\displaystyle\partial_{x}u_{\beta}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\beta e^{\frac{\beta(\beta-1)}{2}t+(\beta-1)\log(x)}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}-\frac{\log(x)}{\sqrt{t}}\right)

(6.69)

\displaystyle\qquad-\beta e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t-(\beta+1)\log(x)}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta+\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}+\frac{\log(x)}{\sqrt{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{x}u_{\beta}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\beta(\beta-1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta-1)}{2}t+(\beta-2)\log(x)}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}-\frac{\log(x)}{\sqrt{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t-(\beta+2)\log(x)}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta+\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}+\frac{\log(x)}{\sqrt{t}}\right)

(6.70)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sqrt{\frac{2\beta^{2}}{\pi t}}e^{-\frac{t}{8}-\frac{3}{2}\log(x)-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2t}}

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{\beta}(t,x)

\displaystyle=Gu_{\beta}(t,x)=\frac{\beta(\beta-1)}{2}e^{\frac{\beta(\beta-1)}{2}t+\beta\log(x)}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}-\frac{\log(x)}{\sqrt{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t-\beta\log(x)}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta+\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}+\frac{\log(x)}{\sqrt{t}}\right)

(6.71)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}e^{-\frac{t}{8}+\frac{\log(x)}{2}-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2t}}.

In particular,

(6.72)

\displaystyle|u_{\beta}(t,x)|

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t+\beta|\log(x)|}

(6.73)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}u_{\beta}(t,x)|

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t+(\beta+1)|\log(x)|}

(6.74)

\displaystyle|\partial^{2}_{x}u_{\beta}(t,x)|

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t+(\beta+2)|\log(x)|}+\frac{\beta\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{\pi t}}e^{\frac{3}{2}|\log(x)|}

(6.75)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}u_{\beta}(t,x)|

\displaystyle=|Gu_{\beta}(t,x)|\leq\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t+\beta|\log(x)|}+\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}e^{\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}.

Now consider the function

(6.76)

\displaystyle q_{\beta}(t,x)=\int_{0}^{t}u_{\beta}(s,x)g_{H}(s;t)\,ds=\int_{0}^{t}u_{\beta}(s,x)\frac{s^{\alpha-1}(t-s)^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(\alpha)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\,ds=\int_{0}^{1}u_{\beta}(st,x)\frac{s^{\alpha-1}(1-s)^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(\alpha)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\,ds.

Similarly to what we did for
q
, we need to prove some regularity results for
q_{\beta}
. We start with the following proposition, concerning the regularity in the
t
variable.

Proposition 6.22
.

Let
{\phi(\lambda)}=\lambda^{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\beta\geq 0
. Then for all
x>0
the function
q_{\beta}(\cdot,x)
belongs to
{\rm AC}(\R_{0}^{+})
and its a.e. derivative
\partial_{t}q_{\beta}(\cdot,x)
satisfies

(6.77)

|\partial_{t}q_{\beta}(t,x)|\leq\alpha\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}(t+1)+\beta|\log(x)|}+\frac{2\beta\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha\right)}{\pi\sqrt{t}\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}.

In particular, for all
t,x>0

\frac{\alpha}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}|P_{s}q_{\beta}(t-s,x)-P_{s}q_{\beta}(t,x)|s^{-1-\alpha}\,ds<\infty

and for all
x>0
and
\lambda>\max\left\{\beta(\beta+1),\frac{3}{4}\right\}

(6.78)

\frac{\alpha}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda t}|P_{s}q_{\beta}(t-s,x)-P_{s}q_{\beta}(t,x)|s^{-1-\alpha}\,ds\,dt<\infty.

Proof.

Arguing as in Proposition
6.13
, let
t>0
and
h\in\left(-\frac{t}{2},1\right)
. We have

(6.79)

\left|\frac{q_{\beta}(t+h,x)-q_{\beta}(t,x)}{h}\right|\leq\int_{0}^{1}\left|\frac{u_{\beta}(st+sh,x)-u_{\beta}(st,x)}{sh}\right|\frac{s^{\alpha}(1-s)^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(\alpha)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\,ds.

By Lagrange’s theorem we know that there exists
\xi\in[\min\{ts,ts+hs\},\max\{ts,ts+hs\}]\subseteq\left[\frac{ts}{2},(t+1)s\right]
such that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{u_{\beta}(st+sh,x)-u_{\beta}(st,x)}{sh}\right|

\displaystyle=|\partial_{t}u_{\beta}(\xi,x)|

\displaystyle\leq\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}\xi+\beta|\log(x)|}+\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2\pi\xi}}e^{\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}(t+1)s+\beta|\log(x)|}+\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{\pi ts}}e^{\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}},

where we used (
6.75
). Using this bound into (
6.79
) we get

\displaystyle\left|\frac{q_{\beta}(t+h,x)-q_{\beta}(t,x)}{h}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}(t+1)+\beta|\log(x)|}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{s^{\alpha}(1-s)^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(\alpha)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{\pi t}}e^{\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{s^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}(1-s)^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(\alpha)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad=\alpha\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}(t+1)+\beta|\log(x)|}+\frac{2\beta\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha\right)}{\pi\sqrt{t}\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}.

Arguing as in Proposition
5.4
, this implies that
q_{\beta}(\cdot,x)
belongs to
{\rm AC}(\R_{0}^{+})
and that its a.e. derivative
\partial_{t}q_{\beta}(t,x)
satisfies (
6.77
).

Next, observe that

\displaystyle|q_{\beta}(t-s,x)-q_{\beta}(t,x)|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{s}|\partial_{t}q(t-\tau,x)|\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}(t+1)+\beta|\log(x)|}s+\frac{2s\beta\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha\right)}{\pi\sqrt{t-s}\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}.

Setting
\widetilde{\beta}=\max\left\{\frac{1}{2},\beta\right\}
, we have that
|q_{\beta}(t-s,\cdot)-q_{\beta}(t,\cdot)|\in\cC_{0}(\widetilde{\beta})
with

\displaystyle[q_{\beta}(t-s,\cdot)-q_{\beta}(t,\cdot)]_{\widetilde{\beta}}\leq\alpha\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}(t+1)}s+\frac{2s\beta\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha\right)}{\pi\sqrt{t-s}\Gamma(\alpha)}

and then, by Item
(5)
of Lemma
6.9

(6.80)

P_{s}|q_{\beta}(t-s,\cdot)-q_{\beta}(t,\cdot)|\\
\leq\left(2\alpha\beta(\beta+1)e^{\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)(t+1)}s+\frac{4s\beta\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha\right)}{\pi\sqrt{t-s}\Gamma(\alpha)}e^{\frac{\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)}{2}t}\right)e^{\widetilde{\beta}|\log(x)|}.

Thus we have

\frac{\alpha}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}|P_{s}q_{\beta}(t-s,x)-P_{s}q_{\beta}(t,x)|s^{-1-\alpha}\,ds\\
\leq\left(\frac{2\alpha^{2}\beta(\beta+1)}{\Gamma(2-\alpha)}e^{\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)(t+1)}t^{1-\alpha}+\frac{4\alpha\beta\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha\right)}{\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma(\alpha)\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-\alpha\right)}e^{\frac{\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)}{2}t}t^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}\right)e^{\widetilde{\beta}|\log(x)|}

that also implies (
6.78
) with
\lambda>\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)
.
∎

Next, we need to study the regularity of
q_{\beta}
in the
x
variable. Let us first focus on the first derivative.

Proposition 6.23
.

For all
t,x>0
, we have

(6.81)

\partial_{x}q_{\beta}(t,x)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{x}u_{\beta}(t\tau,x)\tau^{\alpha-1}(1-\tau)^{-\alpha}d\tau.

Furthermore,
q_{\beta}\in\cC_{0}(\beta)
and
\partial_{x}q_{\beta}\in\cC_{0}(\beta+1)
, with

(6.82)

\displaystyle[q_{\beta}(t,\cdot)]_{\beta}\leq 2e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t}

(6.83)

\displaystyle[\partial_{x}q_{\beta}(t,\cdot)]_{\beta}\leq 2\beta e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t}.

Proof.

The identity (
6.81
) follows by (
6.76
) by a simple application of the dominated convergence theorem, which is justified by (
6.73
). Furthermore, (
6.72
) and (
6.73
) imply respectively (
6.82
) and (
6.83
).
∎

For the second derivative, we need a further assumption.

Proposition 6.24
.

Let
\alpha\in\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right)
and
\beta\geq 0
. Then, for all
t,x>0
, we have

(6.84)

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{x}q_{\beta}(t,x)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{1}\partial^{2}_{x}u_{\beta}(t\tau,x)\tau^{\alpha-1}(1-\tau)^{-\alpha}d\tau.

(6.85)

\displaystyle Gq_{\beta}(t,x)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\alpha)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{1}Gu_{\beta}(t\tau,x)\tau^{\alpha-1}(1-\tau)^{-\alpha}d\tau.

Moreover,
Gq_{\beta}\in\cC_{0}(\widetilde{\beta})
with
\widetilde{\beta}=\max\left\{\beta,\frac{1}{2}\right\}
and

(6.86)

\displaystyle[Gq_{\beta}(t,\cdot)]_{\widetilde{\beta}}\leq\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t}+\frac{\beta\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\pi\sqrt{2t}}.

As a consequence, for all
t,x>0
,

\frac{\alpha}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}|P_{s}q(t,x)-q(t,x)|s^{-1-\alpha}\,ds<\infty,

and for all
x>0
and
\lambda>\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)
,

(6.87)

\frac{\alpha}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda t}|P_{s}q(t,x)-q(t,x)|s^{-1-\alpha}\,ds\,dt<\infty.

Proof.

Fix
x,t>0
and let
0<a<x<b
. For
y\in[a,b]
and
\tau\in[0,1]
, by (
6.74
), we have

|\partial_{x}^{2}u_{\beta}(t\tau,y)|\leq 2\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t+(\beta+2)M}+\frac{\beta\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{\pi t\tau}}e^{\frac{3M}{2}},

where
M=\max\{|\log(a)|,|\log(b)|\}
. Notice that the right-hand side is integrable against
g_{H}(\tau,1)
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle\left(2\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t+(\beta+2)M}+\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2\pi t\tau}}e^{\frac{M}{2}}\right)\tau^{\alpha-1}(1-\tau)^{-\alpha}\,d\tau

\displaystyle=2\beta(\beta+1)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t+(\beta+2)M}+\frac{\beta\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma(1-\alpha)\sqrt{2}}{\pi\sqrt{t}}e^{\frac{3M}{2}}.

Hence, an application of the dominated convergence theorem to (
6.81
) leads to (
6.84
). Furthermore, multiplying both sides of (
6.84
) by
\frac{x^{2}}{2}
we get (
6.85
) and (
6.86
) follows by (
6.85
) and (
6.75
).

Now observe that by Proposition
6.24
we know that
q_{\beta}(t,\cdot),\partial_{x}q_{\beta}(t,\cdot)\in\cC_{0}
, while we also proved that
Gq_{\beta}(t,\cdot)\in\cC_{0}
. Hence we can write

P_{s}q_{\beta}(t,x)-q_{\beta}(t,x)=\int_{0}^{s}GP_{\tau}q_{\beta}(t,x)\,d\tau=\int_{0}^{s}P_{\tau}Gq_{\beta}(t,x)\,d\tau.

Furthermore, by (
6.86
) and Item
(5)
of Lemma
6.9
we get for
\tau\in[0,s]\subseteq[0,t]

(6.88)

\displaystyle P_{\tau}|Gq_{\beta}(t,\cdot)|\leq\left(\beta(\beta+1)e^{\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)t}+\frac{\beta\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\pi\sqrt{2t}}e^{\frac{\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)}{2}t}\right)e^{\widetilde{\beta}|\log(x)|}.

Thus, we have

(6.89)

|P_{s}q_{\beta}(t,x)-q(t,x)|\\
\leq\left(\beta(\beta+1)e^{\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)t}+\frac{\beta\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\pi\sqrt{2t}}e^{\frac{\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)}{2}t}\right)se^{\widetilde{\beta}|\log(x)|}.

and then

\frac{\alpha}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}|P_{s}q_{\beta}(t,x)-q(t,x)|s^{-1-\alpha}\,ds\\
\leq\frac{\alpha}{\Gamma(2-\alpha)}\left(\beta(\beta+1)e^{\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)t}+\frac{\beta\Gamma\left(\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\pi\sqrt{2t}}e^{\frac{\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)}{2}t}\right)t^{1-\alpha}e^{\widetilde{\beta}|\log(x)|}.

Eventually, the latter inequality implies (
6.87
) for
\lambda>\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)
.
∎

Now we check that
q_{\beta}
satisfies Item (iii) of Theorem
3.3
.

Proposition 6.25
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\beta\geq 0
. Then, for all
\lambda>\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}
and
x>0
we have

\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-\alpha}e^{-\lambda t}|q_{\beta}(t,x)|\,dt<\infty.

Proof.

By (
6.82
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-\alpha}e^{-\lambda t}|q_{\beta}(t,x)|\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2e^{\beta|\log(x)|}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-\alpha}e^{-\left(\lambda-\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}\right)t}\,dt

\displaystyle=2e^{\beta|\log(x)|}\left(\lambda-\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}\right)^{\alpha-1}.

∎

We can now prove that
q_{\beta}
is a solution of a special fully nonlocal equation.

Proposition 6.26
.

Let
\alpha\in\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right)
and
\beta\geq 0
. Then
q_{\beta}
is a solution of

(6.90)

\begin{cases}(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q_{\beta}(t,x)=\frac{t^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}q_{\beta}(0,x)&t,x>0\\
q_{\beta}(0,x)=e^{\beta|\log(x)|}&x>0.\end{cases}

Proof.

First notice that
q_{\beta}(0,x)=u_{\beta}(0,x)=e^{\beta|\log(x)|}
. By Propositions
6.22
,
6.24
and
6.25
we know that
(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q_{\beta}(t,x)
is well-defined for all
t,x>0
and for fixed
x>0
the function
(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q_{\beta}(\cdot,x)
is Laplace transformable for
\lambda>\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)
, where
\widetilde{\beta}=\max\left\{\beta,\frac{1}{2}\right\}
. Now we show that

(6.91)

\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q_{\beta}(t,x)\,dt=\lambda^{\alpha-1}e^{\beta|\log(x)|}

for
\lambda>\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{1}{4}+\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)\right)
. This is done exactly as in Proposition
6.19
, the only difference being the bound we prove to use Fubini’s theorem in (
6.56
). Precisely, recall that
f_{\beta}(x)=e^{\beta|\log(x)|}
and set

\overline{u}(t,x)=e^{-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2t}}f_{\widetilde{\beta}}(x)

and notice that, by (
6.72
) and (
6.75
)

\left|(G-\lambda)u_{\beta}(w,y)\right|\leq\left((\beta(\beta+1)+2\lambda)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}w}f_{\widetilde{\beta}}(y)+\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2\pi w}}\overline{u}(w,y)\right).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{s}\tau^{\alpha-1}s^{-\alpha-1}e^{-\lambda(\tau+w)}\left|(G-\lambda)u_{\beta}(w,y)\right|p_{\rm GBM}(\tau,y;x)\,dw\,dy\,d\tau\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{s}(\beta(\beta+1)+2\lambda)e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}w}\tau^{\alpha-1}s^{-\alpha-1}e^{-\lambda(\tau+w)}u_{\widetilde{\beta}}(\tau,x)\,dw\,d\tau\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2\pi w}}\tau^{\alpha-1}s^{-\alpha-1}e^{-\lambda(\tau+w)}P_{\tau}\overline{u}(w,x)\,dw\,d\tau\,ds

(6.92)

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}.

Concerning
I_{1}
, we have, by Item
(5)
of Lemma
6.9

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2(\beta(\beta+1)+2\lambda)\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{s}e^{-\left(\lambda-\frac{\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)}{2}\right)(\tau+w)}\tau^{\alpha-1}s^{-\alpha-1}\,dw\,d\tau\,ds

\displaystyle=\frac{4(\beta(\beta+1)+2\lambda)}{2\lambda-\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)}\left(\int_{0}^{+\infty}\left(1-e^{-\left(\lambda-\frac{\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)}{2}\right)s}\right)s^{-\alpha-1}\,ds\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\left(\lambda-\frac{\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)}{2}\right)\tau}\tau^{\alpha-1}\,d\tau\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{4(\beta(\beta+1)+2\lambda)\Gamma(1-\alpha)\Gamma(\alpha)}{2\lambda-\widetilde{\beta}(\widetilde{\beta}+1)}.

For
I_{2}
, we first need to evaluate
P_{\tau}\overline{u}(w,x)
. We have

\displaystyle P_{\tau}\overline{u}(w,x)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\frac{\log^{2}(y)}{2w}+\widetilde{\beta}|\log(y)|-\frac{\tau}{8}-\frac{(\log(y)-\log(x))^{2}}{2\tau}}\sqrt{\frac{x}{y^{3}2\pi\tau}}\,dy

\displaystyle=\sqrt{x}e^{-\frac{\tau}{8}-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2\tau}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\frac{\log^{2}(y)}{2w}-\frac{\log^{2}(y)}{2\tau}+\frac{\log(y)\log(x)}{\tau}+\widetilde{\beta}|\log(y)|}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y^{3}2\pi\tau}}\,dy.

Now let
z=\log(y)
to achieve

\displaystyle P_{\tau}\overline{u}(w,x)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{x}{2\pi\tau}}e^{-\frac{\tau}{8}-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2\tau}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{2w}-\frac{z^{2}}{2\tau}+\frac{\log(x)}{\tau}z-\frac{z}{2}+\widetilde{\beta}|z|}\,dz.

Define

(6.93)

\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)=\left(\frac{1}{w}+\frac{1}{\tau}\right)^{-1}

so that

\displaystyle P_{\tau}\overline{u}(w,x)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{x\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)}{\tau}}e^{-\frac{\tau}{8}-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2\tau}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)}}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{2\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)}+\left(\frac{\log(x)}{\tau}-\frac{1}{2}\right)z+\widetilde{\beta}|z|}\,dz

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{x\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)}{\tau}}e^{-\frac{\tau}{8}-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2\tau}}\E^{(0,0)}[e^{\widetilde{\beta}|B(\mathcal{H}(\tau,w))|+\left(\frac{\log(x)}{\tau}-\frac{1}{2}\right)B(\mathcal{H}(\tau,w))}]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{x\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)}{\tau}}e^{-\frac{\tau}{8}-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2\tau}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\left(\widetilde{\beta}+\frac{\log(x)}{\tau}-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)}\Phi\left(-\left(\widetilde{\beta}+\frac{\log(x)}{\tau}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{\frac{x\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)}{\tau}}e^{-\frac{\tau}{8}-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2\tau}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\left(\widetilde{\beta}-\frac{\log(x)}{\tau}+\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)}\Phi\left(-\left(\widetilde{\beta}-\frac{\log(x)}{\tau}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{x\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)}{\tau}}e^{-\frac{\tau}{8}-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2(\tau+w)}+A_{1}\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)+\left(\widetilde{\beta}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\frac{w}{\tau+w}\log(x)}\Phi\left(-A_{2}\sqrt{t}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{\frac{x\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)}{\tau}}e^{-\frac{\tau}{8}-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2(\tau+w)}+B_{1}\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)-\left(\widetilde{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\frac{w}{\tau+w}\log(x)}\Phi\left(-B_{2}\sqrt{t}\right),

where

\displaystyle A_{1}=\frac{\widetilde{\beta}^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{8}-\frac{\widetilde{\beta}}{2}

\displaystyle A_{2}=\widetilde{\beta}+\frac{\log(x)}{\tau}-\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle B_{1}=\frac{\widetilde{\beta}^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{8}+\frac{\widetilde{\beta}}{2}

\displaystyle B_{2}=\beta-\frac{\log(x)}{\tau}+\frac{1}{2}

and we used Lemma
6.8
. Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2\pi w}}P_{\tau}\overline{u}(w,x)

\displaystyle=\beta\sqrt{\frac{x}{2\pi(\tau+w)}}e^{-\frac{\tau}{8}-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2(\tau+w)}+A_{1}\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)+\left(\widetilde{\beta}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\frac{w}{\tau+w}\log(x)}\Phi\left(-A_{2}\sqrt{t}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\beta\sqrt{\frac{x}{2\pi(\tau+w)}}e^{-\frac{\tau}{8}-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2(\tau+w)}+B_{1}\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)-\left(\widetilde{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\frac{w}{\tau+w}\log(x)}\Phi\left(-B_{2}\sqrt{t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2\pi(\tau+w)}}e^{-\frac{\tau}{8}-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2(\tau+w)}+B_{1}\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)+\left(\widetilde{\beta}+1\right)|\log(x)|},

where we used
B_{1}>A_{1}
and
\frac{w}{\tau+w}\leq 1
. Now recall that the function
r\in\R^{+}\mapsto\sqrt{r}e^{-\frac{\log^{2}(x)}{2r}}\in\R^{+}
admits as maximum point
r_{\star}=\log^{-2}(x)
, so that

\displaystyle\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2\pi w}}P_{\tau}\overline{u}(w,x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2\pi e}}|\log^{-1}(x)|e^{-\frac{\tau}{8}+B_{1}\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)+\left(\widetilde{\beta}+1\right)|\log(x)|}.

Next, we observe that
2\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)
is the harmonic mean of
\tau
and
w
, hence, we can use the fact that the harmonic mean is smaller than or equal to the arithmetic mean to write

\mathcal{H}(\tau,w)\leq\frac{\tau+w}{4}

and thus we get

\displaystyle\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2\pi w}}P_{\tau}\overline{u}(w,x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2\pi e}}|\log^{-1}(x)|e^{\frac{B_{1}}{4}(\tau+w)+\left(\widetilde{\beta}+1\right)|\log(x)|}.

Now we can go back to
I_{2}
to get

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2\pi e}}|\log^{-1}(x)|e^{\left(\widetilde{\beta}+1\right)|\log(x)|}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{s}\tau^{\alpha-1}s^{-\alpha-1}e^{-\left(\lambda-\frac{B_{1}}{4}\right)(\tau+w)}\,dw\,d\tau\,ds

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta}{\left(\lambda-\frac{B_{1}}{4}\right)\sqrt{2\pi e}}|\log^{-1}(x)|e^{\left(\widetilde{\beta}+1\right)|\log(x)|}\left(\int_{0}^{+\infty}s^{-\alpha-1}\left(1-e^{-\left(\lambda-\frac{B_{1}}{4}\right)s}\right)\,ds\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\int_{0}^{+\infty}\tau^{\alpha-1}e^{-\left(\lambda-\frac{B_{1}}{4}\right)\tau}\,d\tau\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta\Gamma(1-\alpha)\Gamma(\alpha)}{\left(\lambda-\frac{B_{1}}{4}\right)\sqrt{2\pi e}}|\log^{-1}(x)|e^{\left(\widetilde{\beta}+1\right)|\log(x)|}.

Once we have (
6.91
), we know that for all
x>0
there exists
\mathcal{I}_{x}\subset\R^{+}
such that
|\mathcal{I}_{x}|=0
and for all
t\in\R^{+}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{x}
we have

(6.94)

(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q_{\beta}(t,x)=\frac{t^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}q_{\beta}(0,x).

Analogously to what we did in Proposition
6.19
, we need to prove that
\mathcal{I}_{x}=\varnothing
. First, notice that a simple application of the dominated convergence theorem to (
6.76
), justified by (
6.72
), shows that
q_{\beta}
is continuous. Furthermore, arguing exactly as in Proposition
6.19
, by means of the estimates (
6.80
) and (
6.89
), one can show that if
t_{n}\downarrow t
, then
(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q_{\beta}(t_{n},x)\to(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q_{\beta}(t,x)
. This shows, with the same argument as in Proposition
6.19
, that
\mathcal{I}_{x}=\varnothing
for all
x>0
and then
q_{\beta}
is solution of (
6.90
).
∎

Now we want to use the auxiliary function
q_{\beta}
to show that (
6.36
) has a unique solution. To do this, we need also the following lower bound.

Proposition 6.27
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\beta\geq 0
. Then there exists a non-increasing function
C_{\beta}:\R^{+}\to\R
with
C_{\beta}(t)>0
for all
t>0
and such that

q_{\beta}(t,x)\geq C_{\beta}(t)e^{\beta|\log(x)|}.

Proof.

First of all, let us observe that if
x\geq 1
then
\log(x)\geq 0
and we have

u_{\beta}(t,x)\geq e^{\beta\log(x)+\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta+\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}\right)\geq e^{\beta\log(x)+\frac{\beta(\beta-1)}{2}t}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}\right).

If instead
x\in(0,1]
, then
\log(x)\leq 0
and

u_{\beta}(t,x)\geq e^{-\beta\log(x)+\frac{\beta(\beta-1)}{2}t}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}\right).

Hence, in general, we have

u_{\beta}(t,x)\geq e^{\beta|\log(x)|+\frac{\beta(\beta-1)}{2}t}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}\right).

Next, by (
6.76
), we get

\displaystyle q_{\beta}(t,x)

\displaystyle\geq e^{\beta|\log(x)|}\int_{0}^{1}e^{\frac{\beta(\beta-1)}{2}ts}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{ts}\right)\frac{s^{\alpha-1}(1-s)^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(\alpha)\Gamma(1-\alpha)}

\displaystyle\geq\begin{cases}\frac{e^{\beta|\log(x)|+\frac{\beta(\beta-1)}{2}t}}{2}&\beta\leq\frac{1}{2}\\
e^{\beta|\log(x)|+\frac{\beta(\beta-1)}{2}t}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}\right)&\beta\in\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right]\\
e^{\beta|\log(x)|}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}\right)&\beta>1,\end{cases}

that ends the proof.
∎

Now we shall show that the solutions of the fully nonlocal Black-Scholes equation belonging to
\cC_{0,{\rm loc}}(\R_{0}^{+})
are unique.

Proposition 6.28
.

Let
\alpha\in\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right)
and
f\in\cC_{0}
. Then, the equation

(6.95)

\begin{cases}(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}q_{f}(t,x)=\frac{t^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}q_{f}(0,x)&t,x>0\\
q_{f}(0,x)=f(x)&x>0\\
q_{f}\in\cC_{0,{\rm loc}}(\R_{0}^{+})\end{cases}

at most has one solution.

Proof.

Since the equation is linear, it is sufficient to verify that if
f\equiv 0
then the solution
q_{f}\equiv 0
. Fix
T>0
and recall that since
q_{f}\in\cC_{0,{\rm loc}}(\R_{0}^{+})
then

|q_{f}(t,x)|\leq C_{T}e^{\beta_{T}|\log(x)|}.

For simplicity, set
C_{T}=C
and
\beta_{T}:=\beta_{0}
. Now, for any
A\geq 1
and
\beta>\beta_{0}
, consider the function

w_{\beta}(t,x;A):=\frac{C}{C_{\beta}(T)}e^{(\beta_{0}-\beta)|\log(A)|}q_{\beta}(t,x).

Notice that
w_{\beta}(0,x;A)>0=q_{f}(0,x)
for all
x>0
. Next, by Proposition
6.27
we have that, for
t\in(0,T]
,

w_{\beta}(t,x;A)\geq\frac{C_{\beta}(t)}{C_{\beta}(T)}Ce^{(\beta_{0}-\beta)|\log(A)|+\beta|\log(x)|}\geq Ce^{(\beta_{0}-\beta)|\log(A)|+\beta|\log(x)|}.

If
x\not\in\left[A^{-1},A\right]
then
|\log(x)|\geq|\log(A)|
and we have

w_{\beta}(t,x;A)\geq Ce^{\beta_{0}|\log(A)|}\geq|q_{f}(t,x)|.

This shows that for all
x\not\in\left[A^{-1},A\right]
and
t\in[0,T]

(6.96)

w_{\beta}(t,x;A)+q_{f}(t,x)\leq 0\leq w_{\beta}(t,x;A)-q_{f}(t,x).

We want to show that this inequality holds for all
x>0
. To do this, assume by contradiction that there exists a point
(t_{\star},x_{\star})\in[0,T]\times\R^{+}
such that

w_{\beta}(t_{\star},x_{\star};A)-q_{\beta}(t_{\star},x_{\star})<0.

It must hold
x_{\star}\in[A^{-1},A]
. Furthermore, since all the involved functions are continuous, we can assume without loss of generality that

w_{\beta}(t_{\star},x_{\star};A)-q_{f}(t_{\star},x_{\star})=\min_{(t,x)\in[0,T]\times[A^{-1},A]}(w_{\beta}(t,x;A)-q_{f}(t,x)).

First of all, notice that for all
x\in\R^{+}

w_{\beta}(t_{\star},x_{\star};A)-q_{f}(t_{\star},x_{\star})<0<w_{\beta}(0,x;A)-q_{f}(0,x).

Moreover, using (
6.96
) for
x\not\in[A^{-1},A]
and the definition of
(t_{\star},x_{\star})
, if
x\in[A^{-1},A]
, we get

w_{\beta}(t_{\star},x_{\star};A)-q_{f}(t_{\star},x_{\star})\leq w_{\beta}(t,x;A)-q_{f}(t,x)

for all
t\in[0,T]
and
x>0
. By Proposition
3.5
we must have

-(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}(w_{\beta}(\cdot,\cdot;A)-q_{f}(\cdot,\cdot))(x_{\star},t_{\star})+\frac{t_{\star}^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}w_{\beta}(0,x_{\star};A)>0.

However, by Proposition
6.26
we know that, up to a multiplicative constant,
w_{\beta}(\cdot,\cdot;A)
solves (
6.90
), while
q_{f}
solves (
6.95
), hence

-(\partial_{t}-G)^{\alpha}(w_{\beta}(\cdot,\cdot;A)-q_{f}(\cdot,\cdot))(x_{\star},t_{\star})+\frac{t_{\star}^{-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}w_{\beta}(0,x_{\star};A)=0,

which is a contradiction. Thus, we find

w_{\beta}(t,x;A)-q_{f}(t,x)\geq 0,\ \forall x>0,\ t\in[0,T]

With a similar argument one can prove that

w_{\beta}(t,x;A)+q_{f}(t,x)\leq 0,\ \forall x>0,\ t\in[0,T]

and then, since (
6.96
) holds for all
x>0
and
t\in[0,T]
, we have

|q_{f}(t,x)|\leq w_{\beta}(t,x;A)=\frac{C}{C_{\beta}(T)}e^{(\beta_{0}-\beta)|\log(A)|}q_{\beta}(t,x)

taking the limit as
A\to\infty
we get that
|q_{f}(t,x)|=0
for all
x>0
and
t\in[0,T]
. Since
T>0
is arbitrary, we end the proof.
∎

6.3.
Proof of Theorem
6.11

Before proving the main theorem of the section, we must state the last preliminary result whose proof is in Appendix
D
.

Proposition 6.29
.

Let
{\phi(\lambda)}=\lambda^{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Then, on
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\R\times\R^{+})
the generator
A^{\phi}
of
(B^{\phi},{S})
acts as follows:

\displaystyle A^{\phi}f(x,v)

\displaystyle=\int_{\R}\int_{\R}(f(x+h,s+v)-f(x,v)-h\partial_{x}f(x,v)\mathds{1}_{[-1,1]}(h))\mathcal{K}(dh;ds)\,dh,

where
\mathcal{K}
is defined in (
6.34
).

Now we are finally ready to prove Theorem
6.11
.

Proof of Theorem
6.11
.

We already know by (
6.19
) that
q_{\star}(T-t,x,0)=q(t,x)
. Furthermore, by Proposition
6.19
we know that the function
q
solves (
6.36
) and by Proposition
6.28
that it is actually the unique solution. We only need to prove (
6.35
). To do this, denote by
(\mathcal{Q}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
the transition semigroup of
(X_{\rm e}(t),\gamma(t))
and observe that

\displaystyle q_{\star}(t,X(t),\gamma(t))

\displaystyle=\widetilde{Q}_{T-t}u(X(t),\gamma(t))

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\E}^{(x,v)}[(X(T)-K)_{+}\mid X(t),\gamma(t)]

\displaystyle=\E^{(x,v)}[(e^{X_{\rm e}(T)-\frac{T-v-\gamma(T)}{2}}-K)_{+}\mid X(t),\gamma(t)]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Q}_{T-t}g_{T,v}(X_{\rm e}(t),\gamma(t))=\mathcal{Q}_{T-t}g_{T,v}(\log(X(t)),\gamma(t)),

where

g_{T,v}(x,w)=(e^{x-\frac{T-v-w}{2}}-K)_{+}.

By Proposition
6.29
and
[
47
, Theorem 4.1]
, we have, for
w>0

\mathcal{Q}_{t}g(x,w)=g(x,w+t)\mathcal{K}_{w}(x;\R^{d}\times[w+t,\infty))+\int_{\R\times[w,w+t)}\mathcal{Q}_{t+w-\tau}g(x+y,0)\mathcal{K}_{w}(d\tau,dy),

In our case, this becomes

(6.97)

q_{\star}(t,x,w)=\mathcal{Q}_{T-t}g_{T,v}(\log(x),w)=g_{T,v}(\log(x),w+T-t)\mathcal{K}_{w}(x;\R^{d}\times[w+T-t,\infty))\\
+\int_{\R\times[w,w+T-t)}\mathcal{Q}_{T-t+w-\tau}g_{T,v}(\log(x)+y,0)\mathcal{K}_{w}(d\tau,dy).

Now, we just have to evaluate the inner term. We have, setting for brevity
\widetilde{x}=\log(x)+y+t-w+\tau-v
and
\widetilde{t}=T-t+w-\tau
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{T-t+w-\tau}g_{T,v}(\log(x)+y,0)

\displaystyle=\E^{(\log(x)+y,0)}\left[\left(\exp\left(X_{\rm e}(\widetilde{t})-\frac{T-v-\gamma(\widetilde{t})}{2}\right)-K\right)_{+}\right]

\displaystyle=\E^{(\widetilde{x},0)}\left[\left(\exp\left(X_{\rm e}(\widetilde{t})-\frac{\widetilde{t}-\gamma(\widetilde{t})}{2}\right)-K\right)_{+}\right]

\displaystyle=\E^{(\widetilde{x},0)}[(X(\widetilde{t})e^{-\frac{\widetilde{t}-\gamma(\widetilde{t})}{2}}-K)_{+}]=q(\widetilde{t},\widetilde{x}).

Substituting the latter expression into (
6.97
) we get (
6.35
).
∎

Appendix A
A simple property of conditional expectations

Lemma A.1
.

Let
X,Y,Z
be three random variables with values respectively in
E_{X},E_{Y},E_{Z}
. Assume that
Z
is independent of both
X
and
Y
. Then, for any bounded measurable function
F:E_{X}\times E_{Z}\to\R

\E[F(X,Z)\mid X,Y]=\E[F(X,Z)\mid X]

Proof.

First consider two sets
B_{X}\in\cE_{X}
and
B_{Z}\in\cE_{Z}
. Then

\displaystyle\E\left[\mathds{1}_{B_{X}}(X)\mathds{1}_{B_{Z}}(Z)\mid X,Y\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{B_{X}}(X)\E\left[\mathds{1}_{B_{Z}}(Z)\mid X,Y\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{B_{X}}(X)\E\left[\mathds{1}_{B_{Z}}(Z)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{B_{X}}(X)\E\left[\mathds{1}_{B_{Z}}(Z)\mid X\right]

\displaystyle=\E\left[\mathds{1}_{B_{X}}(X)\mathds{1}_{B_{Z}}(Z)\mid X\right].

Now fix
F\in\sigma(X,Y)
and define for any
B\in\cE_{X}\otimes\cE_{Z}

\mu_{F}(B)=\E\left[\mathds{1}_{F}\E\left[\mathds{1}_{B}(X,Z)\mid X,Y\right]\right]

and

\nu_{F}(B)=\E\left[\mathds{1}_{F}\E\left[\mathds{1}_{B}(X,Z)\mid X\right]\right].

Notice that on
B=B_{X}\times B_{Z}
for some
B_{X}\in\cE_{X}
and
B_{Z}\in\cE_{Z}
it holds
\mu_{F}(B_{X}\times B_{Z})=\nu_{F}(B_{X}\times B_{Z})
. Since the sets of the form
B_{X}\times B_{Z}
constitute a
\pi
-system generating
\cE_{X}\otimes\cE_{Z}
, this is enough to guarantee that
\mu_{F}=\nu_{F}
. Furthermore, since
F\in\sigma(X,Y)
is arbitrary, this leads to

\E\left[\mathds{1}_{B}(X,Z)\mid X,Y\right]=\E\left[\mathds{1}_{B}(X,Z)\mid X\right]

for all
B\in\cE_{X}\times\cE_{Z}
. Once this has been shown for indicator functions, a standard argument leads to the statement.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of Lemma
6.8

The proof is by direct calculation. Just notice that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}e^{\lambda_{1}|y|+\lambda_{2}y}p(t,x-y)dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}e^{\lambda_{1}|y|+\lambda_{2}y-\frac{y^{2}}{2t}-\frac{x^{2}}{2t}+\frac{xy}{t}}dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{\lambda_{1}y+\lambda_{2}y-\frac{y^{2}}{2t}-\frac{x^{2}}{2t}+\frac{xy}{t}}dy

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-\lambda_{1}y+\lambda_{2}y-\frac{y^{2}}{2t}-\frac{x^{2}}{2t}+\frac{xy}{t}}dy

\displaystyle=F(t,x;\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})+F(t,-x;\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})

where

F(t,x;\lambda)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{\lambda y-\frac{y^{2}}{2t}-\frac{x^{2}}{2t}+\frac{xy}{t}}dy.

We only need to evaluate
F
. To do this, notice that

\displaystyle\lambda y-\frac{y^{2}}{2t}-\frac{x^{2}}{2t}+\frac{xy}{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(y-(\lambda t+x))^{2}}{2t}+\frac{\lambda(\lambda t+2x)}{2}

hence

F(t,x;\lambda)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}e^{\frac{\lambda(\lambda t+2x)}{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\frac{(y-(\lambda t+x))^{2}}{2t}}dy.

Using the change of variables
v=\frac{y-(\lambda t+x)}{\sqrt{t}}
, we have

F(t,x;\lambda)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{\frac{\lambda(\lambda t+2x)}{2}}\int_{-\frac{\lambda t+x}{\sqrt{t}}}^{+\infty}e^{-v^{2}}{2}dv=e^{\frac{\lambda(\lambda t+2x)}{2}}\Phi\left(-\frac{\lambda t+x}{\sqrt{t}}\right).

This ends the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Proof of Lemma
6.9

We first recall that, by
[
53
, Theorems 6.20 and 8.6]
, the equation

(C.1)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}v(t,y)=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{y}^{2}v(t,y),&t>0,\ x\in\R\\
v(0,y)=g(y),&x\in\R\end{cases}

admits a solution
v_{g}
whenever
g\in C(\R)
satisfies
|g(y)|\leq Ce^{\beta|y|}
for suitable constants
C,\beta>0
and such a solution is unique among functions
v(t,y)
satisfying, for all
T>0
,
|v(t,y)|\leq C_{1}e^{C_{2}y^{2}}
for some constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
and
t\in[0,T]
. Furthermore, still by
[
53
, Theorem 8.6]
, we know that,

v_{g}(t,y)=\E^{(y,0)}[g(B(t))]

Hence, for any
T>0
and
t\in[0,T]
,

|v_{g}(t,y)|\leq\E^{(y,0)}[|g(B(t))|]\leq C\E^{(y,0)}\left[e^{\beta|B(t)|}\right]\leq 2Ce^{\beta|y|+\frac{\beta^{2}t}{2}},

where we also used Lemma
6.8
.
Now consider the equation

(C.2)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}u(t,x)=Gu(t,x),&t>0,\ x\in\R^{+}\\
u(0,x)=f(x)&x\in\R^{+},\end{cases}

where
f\in\cC_{0}
and let
\beta>0
be such that
f\in\cC_{0}(\beta)
. Then if we set

g(y)=e^{-\frac{y}{2}}f(e^{y}),\ x\in\R

we do have
|g(y)|\leq[f]_{\beta}e^{\left(\beta+\frac{1}{2}\right)|y|}
and
g\in C(\R)
, hence we can solve the heat equation (
C.1
), providing the unique solution
v_{g}(t,y)
that satisfies

|v_{g}(t,y)|\leq 2[f]_{\beta}e^{\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta+\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}t+\left(\beta+\frac{1}{2}\right)|y|}.

According to
[
53
, Proposition 7.9]
, we know that

u_{f}(t,x)=e^{\frac{\log(x)}{2}-\frac{t}{8}}v_{g}(t,\log(x))

is a classical solution of (
C.2
). Hence, in particular,
u_{f}\in C(\R_{0}^{+}\times\R^{+})\cap C^{1}(\R^{+}\times\R^{+})
and for all
t>0
it holds
u_{f}(t,\cdot)\in C^{2}(\R^{+})
. Moreover, for
x>0
we get

(C.3)

|u_{f}(t,x)|\leq e^{\frac{|\log(x)|}{2}}|v_{g}(t,\log(x))|\leq 2[f]_{\beta}e^{(\beta+1)|\log(x)|+\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t}

hence
u_{f}\in\cC_{\rm sol}
. To show uniqueness, notice that if we consider any other solution of (
C.2
) in
\cC_{\rm sol}
, then we have, still by
[
53
, Proposition 7.9]
, that
v^{\prime}_{g}(t,x)=e^{-\frac{x}{2}+\frac{t}{8}}u(t,e^{x})
satisfies (
C.1
) with

|v^{\prime}_{g}(t,x)|\leq\widetilde{C}_{T}e^{\frac{T}{8}}e^{\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{T}+\frac{1}{2}\right)|x|}

for all
T>0
,
t\in[0,T]
,
x\in\R
. By
[
53
, Theorem 6.20]
, we know that
v^{\prime}_{g}=v_{g}
hence
u^{\prime}_{f}=u_{f}
.

Denote by
(P_{s})_{s\geq 0}
the semigroup action on
\cC_{0}
such that for all
f\in\cC_{0}
the function
u_{f}(s,x)=P_{s}f(x)
is the unique solution of (
6.33
) in
\cC_{\rm sol}
.

Notice that for the special initial data
f_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}(x)=e^{\beta_{1}|\log(x)|+\beta_{2}\log(x)}
, that belongs to
\cC_{0}(|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|)
, we have
g(y)=e^{\left(\beta_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right)y+\beta_{1}|y|}
, hence, by Lemma
6.8
, it holds

\displaystyle v_{g}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\E^{(y,0)}[g(B(t))]=e^{\left(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right)y+\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}t}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}-\frac{y}{\sqrt{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+e^{-\left(\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)y+\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}t}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}+\frac{y}{\sqrt{t}}\right).

As a consequence, we get, in such a case

\displaystyle P_{t}f_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}(x)

\displaystyle=e^{(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2})\log(x)+\frac{(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2})(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}-1)}{2}t}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}-\frac{\log(x)}{\sqrt{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+e^{-(\beta_{1}-\beta_{2})\log(x)+\frac{(\beta_{1}-\beta_{2})(\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}+1)}{2}t}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}+\frac{\log(x)}{\sqrt{t}}\right).

This shows Item
(4)
.

Now we prove Item
(1)
. Recall that for
f\in\cC_{0}
it holds
P_{t}f(x)=e^{\frac{\log(x)}{2}-\frac{t}{8}}v_{g}(t,\log(x))
, where
v_{g}
satisfies (
C.1
) with
g(y)=e^{-\frac{y}{2}}f(e^{y})
. As before, we also recall that

v_{g}(t,y)=\int_{\R}g(z)p(t,z-y)dz=\int_{\R}e^{-\frac{z}{2}}f(e^{z})p(t,z-y)dz,

where

p(t,z-y)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}e^{-\frac{(z-y)^{2}}{2t}}

is the heat kernel, as in (
6.25
). Hence,

P_{t}f(x)=e^{\frac{\log(x)}{2}-\frac{t}{8}}\int_{\R}e^{-\frac{z}{2}}f(e^{z})p(t,z-\log(x))dz.

Using the change of variables
z=\log(w)
, we achieve

\displaystyle P_{t}f(x)

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{\log(x)}{2}-\frac{t}{8}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{e^{-\frac{\log(w)}{2}}}{w}f(w)p(t,\log(w)-\log(x))dw

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}f(w)p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)dw.

Since
p_{\rm GBM}(t,\cdot;x)
is a probability density function, this also guarantees that
P_{t}
is positivity preserving and sub-Markov. In particular, if
f\in\cC_{0}(\beta)
, then
|f|\leq f_{\beta,0}=:f_{\beta}
and then

\displaystyle|P_{t}f(x)|

\displaystyle\leq P_{t}|f|(x)\leq[f]_{\beta}P_{t}f_{\beta,0}(x)\leq[f]_{\beta}e^{\beta\log(x)+\frac{\beta(\beta-1)}{2}t}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}-\frac{\log(x)}{\sqrt{t}}\right)

\displaystyle+[f]_{\beta}e^{-\beta\log(x)+\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t}\Phi\left(-\left(\beta+\frac{1}{2}\right)\sqrt{t}+\frac{\log(x)}{\sqrt{t}}\right)\leq 2[f]_{\beta}e^{\frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{2}t+\beta|\log(x)|},

that shows Item
(5)
.

To prove Item
(2)
observe that

p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)=\frac{1}{w\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{\left(2\log(w)-2\log(x)+t\right)^{2}}{8t}\right)

and then

\partial_{x}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)=\frac{2\log(w)-2\log(x)+t}{2txw\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{\left(2\log(w)-2\log(x)+t\right)^{2}}{8t}\right).

Fix
0<a<b
and let
x\in[a,b]
. Let also
\beta\geq 0
be such that
f\in\cC_{0}(\beta)
and set
M=\max\{|\log(a)|,|\log(b)|\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle|f(w)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)|

\displaystyle\leq[f]_{\beta}\frac{|2\log(w)-2\log(x)+t|}{2txw\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(\beta|\log(w)|-\frac{\left(2\log(w)-2\log(x)+t\right)^{2}}{8t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{[f]_{\beta}}{2at\sqrt{2\pi}}g_{t}(\log(w))\exp\left(-\log(w)-\frac{\log^{2}(w)}{16t}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\log^{2}(w)}{32t}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\exp\left(\beta|\log(w)|-\frac{\log^{2}(w)}{8t}\right)\exp\left(\frac{-\log^{2}(w)+2\log(w)(2\log(x)-t)}{4t}\right),

where

g_{t}(z)=(2|z|+2M+t)e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{32t}}.

First, notice that
g_{t}\in C(\R)
,
g_{t}(z)\geq 0
for all
z\in\R
and
\lim_{z\to\pm\infty}g_{t}(z)=0
. Hence, there exists
M_{1}(t)=\max_{z\in\R}g_{t}(z)
. Next, consider the parabola
r\in\R\mapsto-\frac{r^{2}}{8t}+\beta r\in\R
and we notice that its vertex, which is its maximum, lies in the point
\left(4\beta t,2\beta^{2}t\right)
. Similarly, the vertex of the parabola
r\in\R\mapsto-\frac{r^{2}}{4t}+\frac{r(2\log(x)-t)}{2t}\in\R
lies in the point
\left(2\log(x)-t,\frac{(2\log(x)-t)^{2}}{4t}\right)
, which is its maximum. Finally, consider the parabola
r\in\R\mapsto-\frac{r^{2}}{16t}-r\in\R
and notice that its vertex, that is its maximum, lies in the point
\left(-8t,4t\right)
. Hence, if we set

C_{t}:=\frac{[f]_{\beta}M_{1}(t)}{2at\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(2(\beta^{2}+2)t\right)\max_{x\in[a,b]}\exp\left(\frac{(2\log(x)-t)^{2}}{4t}\right)

we get

\displaystyle|f(w)\partial_{x}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)|\leq C_{t}\exp\left(-\frac{\log^{2}(w)}{32t}\right),

where the right-hand side is integrable and independent of
x
. Hence, by dominated convergence,

\partial_{x}P_{t}f(x)=\int_{0}^{+\infty}f(w)\partial_{x}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\,dw.

To prove the bound, we argue as follows: first we notice that

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}P_{t}f(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{+\infty}|f(w)\partial_{x}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)|\,dw

\displaystyle=\frac{[f]_{\beta}}{2tx\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\left|2\log(w)-2\log(x)+t\right|}{w}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp\left(\beta|\log(w)|-\frac{(2\log(w)-2\log(x)+t)^{2}}{8t}\right)\,dw.

Next, we apply the change of variables
z=\frac{2\log(w)-2\log(x)+t}{2\sqrt{t}}
, obtaining

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}P_{t}f(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{[f]_{\beta}}{x\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|z|\exp\left(\beta\left|z\sqrt{t}+\log(x)-\frac{t}{2}\right|-\frac{z^{2}}{2}\right)\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{[f]_{\beta}}{2x\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(\beta\left|\log(x)\right|+\beta\frac{t}{2}\right)\int_{0}^{+\infty}z\exp\left(\beta\sqrt{t}z-\frac{z^{2}}{2}\right)\,dz

\displaystyle=\frac{[f]_{\beta}}{2x\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(\beta\left|\log(x)\right|+\beta\frac{t}{2}\right)\int_{0}^{+\infty}(z-\beta\sqrt{t})\exp\left(\beta\sqrt{t}z-\frac{z^{2}}{2}\right)\,dz

\displaystyle+\frac{[f]_{\beta}\beta\sqrt{t}}{2x\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(\beta\left|\log(x)\right|+\beta\frac{t}{2}\right)\int_{0}^{+\infty}\exp\left(\beta\sqrt{t}z-\frac{z^{2}}{2}\right)\,dz.

\displaystyle:=\frac{2[f]_{\beta}}{x\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(\beta\left|\log(x)\right|+\beta\frac{t}{2}\right)(I_{1}+I_{2}).

Concerning
I_{1}
, it is easy to check that
I_{1}=1
. For
I_{2}
, we rewrite it as

I_{2}=\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}\E^{(0,0)}\left[e^{\beta\sqrt{t}|B(1)|}\right]=\sqrt{2\pi}e^{\frac{\beta^{2}t^{2}}{2}}\Phi\left(-\beta\sqrt{t}\right).

Hence we get

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}P_{t}f(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2[f]_{\beta}}{x\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(\beta\left|\log(x)\right|+\beta\frac{t}{2}\right)\left(1+\sqrt{2\pi}e^{\frac{\beta^{2}t^{2}}{2}}\Phi\left(-\beta\sqrt{t}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4[f]_{\beta}\exp\left((\beta+1)\left|\log(x)\right|+\beta\frac{t}{2}+\frac{\beta^{2}t^{2}}{2}\right),

that proves Item
(2)
.

Finally, we prove Item
(3)
. Let
f
as required, in particular with
f\in\cC_{0}(\beta)
,
\partial_{x}f\in\cC_{0}(\beta^{\prime})
and
Gf\in\cC_{0}(\beta^{\prime\prime}
. Recall that

(C.4)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}P_{t}f(x)\,=\,\int_{0}^{\infty}f(w)\,\partial_{x}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\,dw.

We want to prove a similar property for the second derivative. To do this, first notice that

(C.5)

\partial_{x}^{2}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)=\frac{(-8t+(2\log(w)-2\log(x)+t)^{2}-4t(2\log(w)-2\log(x)+t))}{8t^{2}w\sqrt{2\pi}x^{2}}\\
\times\exp\left(-\frac{(2\log(w)-2\log(x)+t)^{2}}{8t}\right).

By a similar argument as the one adopted in Item
(2)
, we obtain, for fixed
x\in[a,b]
,
0<a<b
, and
t>0
,

(C.6)

\displaystyle|f(x)\partial_{y}^{2}p(t,x;x)|\leq C_{t}\exp\left(-\frac{\log^{2}(w)}{32t}\right)

for some constant
C_{t}
depending on
t
,
a
,
b
,
\beta
and
[f]_{\beta}
. Again, by dominated convergence, we have

(C.7)

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{x}P_{t}f(x)\,=\,\int_{0}^{\infty}f(w)\,\partial^{2}_{x}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\,dw.

Next, notice that

(C.8)

\displaystyle\frac{x^{2}}{2}\partial_{x}^{2}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\,=\,\partial_{w}^{2}\left(\frac{w^{2}}{2}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\right).

Hence we get

(C.9)

\displaystyle GP_{t}f(x)\,=\,\int_{0}^{\infty}f(w)\,\partial^{2}_{w}\left(\frac{w^{2}}{2}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\right)\,dw.

Now we want to integrate by parts. To do this, we first notice that, again with the same exact argument, we have

\left|f(w)\,\partial_{w}\left(\frac{w^{2}}{2}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\right)\right|\leq C\exp\left(-\frac{\log^{2}(w)}{32t}\right)

for some constant
C
depending on
t,x,\beta
and
[f]_{\beta}
. Hence, in particular,

\lim_{w\to 0}\left|f(w)\,\partial_{w}\left(\frac{w^{2}}{2}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\right)\right|=\lim_{w\to+\infty}\left|f(w)\,\partial_{w}\left(\frac{w^{2}}{2}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\right)\right|=0

and then, integrating by parts in (
C.9
),

(C.10)

\displaystyle GP_{t}f(x)\,=\,-\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{w}f(w)\,\partial_{w}\left(\frac{w^{2}}{2}p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\right)\,dw.

To integrate by parts a second time, we notice again that

(C.11)

\frac{w^{2}}{2}\left|\partial_{w}f(w)\,p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\right|\leq C\exp\left(-\frac{\log^{2}(w)}{32t}\right)

for some constant
C
depending on
t,x,\beta^{\prime}
and
[\partial_{x}f]_{\beta^{\prime}}
. Thus, again

\lim_{w\to 0}\frac{w^{2}}{2}\left|\partial_{w}f(w)\,p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\right|=\lim_{w\to+\infty}\frac{w^{2}}{2}\left|\partial_{w}f(w)\,p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\right|=0

and then, integrating by parts in (
C.10
),

(C.12)

\displaystyle GP_{t}f(x)\,=\,-\int_{0}^{\infty}Gf(w)p_{\rm GBM}(t,w;x)\,dw=P_{t}Gf(x),

where the integral on the right-hand side is finite since
Gf\in\cC_{0}
.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of Proposition
6.29

Let us consider the process
(B(t),\tau(t))
, where
B(t)
is a Brownian motion such that
\mathds{P}^{(x,v)}(B(0)=x)=1
and
\mathds{P}^{(x,v)}(\tau(t)=t+v)=1
. Notice that
(B^{\phi},{S})
is obtained from
(B,\tau)
by means of subordination. Furthermore, the semigroup
(\mathcal{T}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
of
(B(t),\tau(t))
is given by

\mathcal{T}_{t}f(x,v)=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}f(y,t+v)p(t,x-y)dy,\forall f\in C_{b}(\R\times\R^{+}_{0}).

where
p
is given by (
6.25
). By Phillips’ Theorem, we know that at least on
C_{c}^{\infty}(\R\times\R^{+})
the generator
A^{\phi}
of
(B^{\phi},\sigma)
is given by

\displaystyle A^{\phi}f(x,v)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{+\infty}(\mathcal{T}_{t}f(x,v)-f(x,v))t^{-1-\alpha}\,dt

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(f(y,t+v)-f(x,v))p(t,x-y)t^{-1-\alpha}\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(f(x+h,t+v)-f(x,v))p(t,h)t^{-1-\alpha}\,dh\,dt,

where we applied the change of variables
x-y=h
. Now notice that by symmetry

\int_{-1}^{1}h\partial_{x}f(x,v)p(t,h)\,dh=0

hence we can rewrite

(D.1)

\displaystyle A^{\phi}f(x,v)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(f(x+h,t+v)-f(x,v)-h\partial_{x}f(x,v)\mathds{1}_{[-1,1]}(h))p(t,h)t^{-1-\alpha}\,dh\,dt.

Now let us first show that the integral is absolutely convergent. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}

\displaystyle|f(x+h,t+v)-f(x,v)-h\partial_{x}f(x,v)|p(t,h)t^{-1-\alpha}\,dh\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|f(x+h,t+v)-f(x+h,v)|p(t,h)t^{-1-\alpha}\,dh\,dt

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|f(x+h,v)-f(x,v)-h\partial_{x}f(x,v)\mathds{1}_{[-1,1]}(h)|p(t,h)t^{-1-\alpha}\,dh\,dt

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}.

As for
I_{1}
, we split the integral in two parts:

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|f(x+h,t+v)-f(x+h,v)|p(t,h)t^{-1-\alpha}\,dh\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{1}^{+\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|f(x+h,t+v)-f(x+h,v)|p(t,h)t^{-1-\alpha}\,dh\,dt=I_{3}+I_{4}.

Since
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\R\times\R^{+})
and
\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}p(t,h)dh=1
we have

I_{3}\leq\max_{(x,v)\in\R\times\R^{+}}|\partial_{v}f(x,v)|\int_{0}^{1}t^{-\alpha}\,dt=\frac{\max_{(x,v)\in\R\times\R^{+}}|\partial_{v}f(x,v)|}{1-\alpha}

while

I_{4}\leq 2\max_{(x,v)\in\R\times\R^{+}}|f(x,v)|\int_{1}^{+\infty}t^{-1-\alpha}\,dt=\frac{\max_{(x,v)\in\R\times\R^{+}}|f(x,v)|}{\alpha}.

To handle
I_{2}
, we first change the order of the integral, defining

j(h):=\int_{0}^{+\infty}p(t,h)t^{-1-\alpha}dt=C_{\alpha}|h|^{1+2\alpha}

for some normalizing constant
C_{\alpha}>0
. Then we split the integral in two parts:

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{h\not\in[-1,1]}|f(x+h,v)-f(x,v)|j(h)\,dh\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{h\in[-1,1]}|f(x+h,v)-f(x,v)-h\partial_{x}f(x,v)|j(h)\,dh\,dt=I_{5}+I_{6}.

For
I_{5}
, we have

I_{5}\leq 4\max_{(x,v)\in\R\times\R^{+}}|f(x,v)|\int_{1}^{+\infty}h^{-1-2\alpha}\,dh=\frac{4\max_{(x,v)\in\R\times\R^{+}}}{2\alpha},

while to handle
I_{6}
we observe that

I_{6}\leq\max_{(x,v)\in\R\times\R^{+}}|\partial_{x}^{2}f(x,v)|\int_{0}^{1}h^{1-2\alpha}\,dh=\frac{\max_{(x,v)\in\R\times\R^{+}}|\partial_{x}^{2}f(x,v)|}{2(1-\alpha)}.

Now we want to find an alternative way of writing (
D.1
). To do this, we define the following family of measures on
\R
:

A\in\cB(\R)\mapsto\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}(A,h)=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathds{1}_{A}(t)p(t,h)\frac{\alpha t^{-1-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\,dt.

We can then define a further measure on
\R^{2}
by setting for any
A_{1},A_{2}\in\cB(\R)

\mathcal{K}(A_{1}\times A_{2})=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\mathds{1}_{A_{1}}(h)\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}(A_{2},h)\,dh.

On the other hand, notice that (
D.1
) can be rewritten as

A^{\phi}f(x,v)=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{\R}\frac{\alpha t^{-1-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}(f(x+h,s+v)-f(x,v)-h\partial_{x}f(x,v)\mathds{1}_{[-1,1]}(h))p(t,h)\,\delta_{t}(ds)\,dt\,dh.

However, observe that for any
A\in\cB(\R)
it holds

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}(A,h)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathds{1}_{A}(t)p(t,h)\frac{\alpha t^{-1-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\,dt=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\delta_{t}(A)p(t,h)\frac{\alpha t^{-1-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{\R}\mathds{1}_{A}(s)p(t,h)\frac{\alpha t^{-1-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\,\delta_{t}(ds)\,dt.

By a standard measure theory argument we can conclude that

\displaystyle A^{\phi}f(x,v)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\int_{\R}(f(x+h,s+v)-f(x,v)-h\partial_{x}f(x,v)\mathds{1}_{[-1,1]}(h))\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}(ds;h)\,dh

\displaystyle=\int_{\R}\int_{\R}(f(x+h,s+v)-f(x,v)-h\partial_{x}f(x,v)\mathds{1}_{[-1,1]}(h))\mathcal{K}(dh;ds)\,dh,

where the latter follows by definition of the measure
K
.
∎
